It's just hard to sit there and wait.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with restlessness, fidgeting, and sustaining attention are significantly interfering with your overall quality of life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say it is. Or, you know, if I'm in a meeting, I'll catch myself clicking a pen over and over. I don't even really notice I'm doing it until someone looks at me or I suddenly realize it myself. It's just this constant need to be doing something small like that.\n\nDoctor: Has your difficulty sitting still or constant need to be moving affected your relationships with coworkers or your performance at work?\n\nPatient: Well, it hasn't really caused problems with my coworkers personally, and I generally get along pretty well with the customers. But, um, it has started affecting my performance, yeah. It's actually been showing up in my recent performance reviews. My supervisors have been pointing out that I have a lot of unfinished tickets, and, you know, I make these little mistakes that end up slowing the whole team down. It's frustrating because I really am trying hard, but it just keeps happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your constant need to be moving and difficulty sitting still are causing you a lot of stress or anxiety?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, it's annoying, sure, especially when someone points it out, but I wouldn't say it causes me a lot of stress or anxiety. It's just sort of... how I am. The performance reviews and the mistakes, that stresses me out a bit, but the actual fidgeting itself doesn't really bother me emotionally.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble sitting still or feel restless when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Yeah, looking back, this has been around long before I started this customer service job. Even when I was a kid in grade school, it was an issue. My teachers used to write on my report cards that I was bright, but careless. I was always talking out of turn, and they constantly had to remind me to just sit still and stay with my work. So, yeah, it's been going on for a long time.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty sustaining attention in tasks or activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do struggle with that. Like, in the longer coaching meetings at work, or when I have to read through a new procedure, I usually start off fine. But after a few minutes, I'll suddenly realize that my eyes have just been moving across the page, or I've been sitting there, and I haven't actually taken in a word of what was said or what I just read.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel impatient or irritated when you are forced to wait or when things are not happening quickly enough?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do get pretty impatient. Like, when we have team huddles at work or if I'm waiting in the help queue for a supervisor, it makes me really antsy. I always catch myself checking the screen over and over again, and I usually just want to jump in and start talking before it's actually my turn or before I'm called on. It's just hard to sit there and wait.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with restlessness, fidgeting, and sustaining attention are significantly interfering with your overall quality of life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say it is. At work, it's actually started showing up in my performance reviews. My supervisors have been pointing out that I leave tickets unfinished, or I make careless mistakes. It's frustrating because I'm trying really hard, and I generally get along great with the customers, but these mistakes end up slowing the whole team down. So, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your tasks or belongings?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I have much trouble with that, no. I mean, my desk at work is pretty tidy, and I keep my tools and stuff at home where they belong. I might lose track of a pen now and then, but organization isn't really something I struggle with.\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk more than others in social situations?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I talk all day on the phones for work, but when I'm out socially... I don't think I dominate the conversation or anything. I might jump in a little too quick sometimes, like I mentioned before, but I wouldn't say I just talk on and on more than anyone else. I'm probably pretty average in that department.\n\nDoctor: Were you more physically active or restless than your peers when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. Looking back, this whole thing has been around long before my current job. Even back in grade school, I remember my teachers would write on my report cards that I was a bright kid but careless. They'd always say I talked out of turn and constantly had to be reminded to just stay with my work. So, yeah, I guess I've always been a bit more restless than the other kids.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. So I figured I should finally come in and talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say so. I mean, I might make a quick decision sometimes, but I don't really just jump into things without thinking them through. I'm pretty careful about what I do, so that hasn't really been an issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're supposed to be sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely do that. It's like I can't completely stop moving. Even when I'm trying to look calm and professional at work, my foot is usually bouncing under the desk. It's just this constant need to be doing something small like that.\n\nDoctor: Has your difficulty sitting still or constant need to be moving affected your relationships with coworkers or your performance at work?\n\nPatient: Well, it hasn't really caused problems with my coworkers personally, and I generally get along pretty well with the customers. It's frustrating because I really am trying hard, but it just keeps happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your constant need to be moving and difficulty sitting still are causing you a lot of stress or anxiety?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, it's annoying, sure, especially when someone points it out, but I wouldn't say it causes me a lot of stress or anxiety. It's just sort of... how I am. The performance reviews and the mistakes, that stresses me out a bit, but the actual fidgeting itself doesn't really bother me emotionally.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble sitting still or feel restless when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Yeah, looking back, this has been around long before I started this customer service job. Even when I was a kid in grade school, it was an issue. My teachers used to write on my report cards that I was bright, but careless. I was always talking out of turn, and they constantly had to remind me to just sit still and stay with my work. So, yeah, it's been going on for a long time.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty sustaining attention in tasks or activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do struggle with that. Like, in the longer coaching meetings at work, or when I have to read through a new procedure, I usually start off fine. But after a few minutes, I'll suddenly realize that my eyes have just been moving across the page, or I've been sitting there, and I haven't actually taken in a word of what was said or what I just read.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel impatient or irritated when you are forced to wait or when things are not happening quickly enough?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do get pretty impatient. Like, when we have team huddles at work or if I'm waiting in the help queue for a supervisor, it makes me really antsy. I always catch myself checking the screen over and over again, and I usually just want to jump in and start talking before it's actually my turn or before I'm called on. It's just hard to sit there and wait.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with restlessness, fidgeting, and sustaining attention are significantly interfering with your overall quality of life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say it is. At work, it's actually started showing up in my performance reviews. My supervisors have been pointing out that I leave tickets unfinished, or I make careless mistakes. It's frustrating because I'm trying really hard, and I generally get along great with the customers, but these mistakes end up slowing the whole team down. So, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your tasks or belongings?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I have much trouble with that, no. I'm probably pretty average in that department.\n\nDoctor: Were you more physically active or restless than your peers when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, doctor. Well, what brings me in is that I just feel like I'm always moving from one thing to the next. I really can't just idle. And, um, there's a new setup at my work—I do customer service—and it's just made that a lot harder to hide lately. So I figured I should finally come in and talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say so. I mean, I might make a quick decision sometimes, but I don't really just jump into things without thinking them through. I'm pretty careful about what I do, so that hasn't really been an issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're supposed to be sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely do that. It's like I can't completely stop moving. Even when I'm trying to look calm and professional at work, my foot is usually bouncing under the desk. Or, you know, if I'm in a meeting, I'll catch myself clicking a pen over and over. I don't even really notice I'm doing it until someone looks at me or I suddenly realize it myself. It's just this constant need to be doing something small like that.\n\nDoctor: Has your difficulty sitting still or constant need to be moving affected your relationships with coworkers or your performance at work?\n\nPatient: Well, it hasn't really caused problems with my coworkers personally, and I generally get along pretty well with the customers. But, um, it has started affecting my performance, yeah. It's actually been showing up in my recent performance reviews. My supervisors have been pointing out that I have a lot of unfinished tickets, and, you know, I make these little mistakes that end up slowing the whole team down. It's frustrating because I really am trying hard, but it just keeps happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your constant need to be moving and difficulty sitting still are causing you a lot of stress or anxiety?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, it's annoying, sure, especially when someone points it out, but I wouldn't say it causes me a lot of stress or anxiety. It's just sort of... how I am. The performance reviews and the mistakes, that stresses me out a bit, but the actual fidgeting itself doesn't really bother me emotionally.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble sitting still or feel restless when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Yeah, looking back, this has been around long before I started this customer service job. Even when I was a kid in grade school, it was an issue. My teachers used to write on my report cards that I was bright, but careless. I was always talking out of turn, and they constantly had to remind me to just sit still and stay with my work. So, yeah, it's been going on for a long time.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty sustaining attention in tasks or activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do struggle with that. Like, in the longer coaching meetings at work, or when I have to read through a new procedure, I usually start off fine. But after a few minutes, I'll suddenly realize that my eyes have just been moving across the page, or I've been sitting there, and I haven't actually taken in a word of what was said or what I just read.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel impatient or irritated when you are forced to wait or when things are not happening quickly enough?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do get pretty impatient. Like, when we have team huddles at work or if I'm waiting in the help queue for a supervisor, it makes me really antsy. I always catch myself checking the screen over and over again, and I usually just want to jump in and start talking before it's actually my turn or before I'm called on. It's just hard to sit there and wait.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with restlessness, fidgeting, and sustaining attention are significantly interfering with your overall quality of life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say it is. At work, it's actually started showing up in my performance reviews. My supervisors have been pointing out that I leave tickets unfinished, or I make careless mistakes. It's frustrating because I'm trying really hard, and I generally get along great with the customers, but these mistakes end up slowing the whole team down. So, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your tasks or belongings?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I have much trouble with that, no. I mean, my desk at work is pretty tidy, and I keep my tools and stuff at home where they belong. I might lose track of a pen now and then, but organization isn't really something I struggle with.\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk more than others in social situations?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I talk all day on the phones for work, but when I'm out socially... I don't think I dominate the conversation or anything. I might jump in a little too quick sometimes, like I mentioned before, but I wouldn't say I just talk on and on more than anyone else. I'm probably pretty average in that department.\n\nDoctor: Were you more physically active or restless than your peers when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. Looking back, this whole thing has been around long before my current job. Even back in grade school, I remember my teachers would write on my report cards that I was a bright kid but careless. They'd always say I talked out of turn and constantly had to be reminded to just stay with my work. So, yeah, I guess I've always been a bit more restless than the other kids.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had difficulty waiting for your turn, such as in line or in a game?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say so. I notice it a lot at work, actually. Like when we have a team huddle or I'm sitting in the help queue... waiting for my turn just makes me really antsy. I'll catch myself checking the screen over and over again, or I just want to jump in before I'm actually called on. At work, it's actually started showing up in my performance reviews. My supervisors have been pointing out that I leave tickets unfinished, or I make careless mistakes. It's frustrating because I'm trying really hard, and I generally get along great with the customers, but these mistakes end up slowing the whole team down. So, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your tasks or belongings?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I have much trouble with that, no. I mean, my desk at work is pretty tidy, and I keep my tools and stuff at home where they belong. I might lose track of a pen now and then, but organization isn't really something I struggle with.\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk more than others in social situations?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I talk all day on the phones for work, but when I'm out socially... I don't think I dominate the conversation or anything. I might jump in a little too quick sometimes, like I mentioned before, but I wouldn't say I just talk on and on more than anyone else. I'm probably pretty average in that department.\n\nDoctor: Were you more physically active or restless than your peers when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. Looking back, this whole thing has been around long before my current job. Even back in grade school, I remember my teachers would write on my report cards that I was a bright kid but careless. They'd always say I talked out of turn and constantly had to be reminded to just stay with my work. I'll catch myself checking the screen over and over again, or I just want to jump in before I'm actually called on. So, yeah, just sitting there waiting is definitely a bit frustrating for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these difficulties with restlessness and sustaining attention in other areas of your life outside of work?\n\nPatient: Well, it's mostly work where it's really causing problems right now. I mean, it's actually started showing up in my performance reviews. My supervisors keep pointing out that I have unfinished tickets, and they say I'm making careless mistakes. It's frustrating because they tell me it slows the whole team down, even though I'm really trying hard and I generally get along great with the customers. But outside of work... I guess it's there, but the consequences just aren't as serious as getting a bad review at my job.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced difficulties with restlessness and sustaining attention in your personal relationships or daily activities outside of work?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean... I haven't really noticed it causing any major issues at home or with my friends. If I get a little antsy around the house, I can just get up and do something else, you know? There's no one timing me or checking my work like at the office. So, yeah, I wouldn't say it's really a problem in my personal life.\n\nDoctor: Do you often forget things you were supposed to do or remember appointments and meetings?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say so. Like at work... I'll mean to call a customer back, or maybe there's an internal message I need to answer after lunch. But honestly, unless I write it down in more than one place, it can just completely slip my mind until the next day. So, yeah, that definitely happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulty sustaining attention in tasks is more problematic for you than your restlessness and fidgeting?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't really thought about it like that. I mean, they both just kind of cause issues for me at work. The attention part is probably what gets me in trouble with the unfinished tickets and the mistakes, but the restlessness just makes sitting at that desk all day really tough. So... yeah, I don't know if I could say one is definitely a bigger problem than the other. They just sort of go hand in hand.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they are speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do that sometimes. I'll give them the answer before they're even finished explaining the issue, or, you know, before anyone even asked for my help. It just kind of comes out.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced difficulties with restlessness and sustaining attention in your social relationships?\n\nPatient: No, not really. When I'm hanging out with people or, you know, just doing my own thing, it doesn't seem to be an issue.\n\nDoctor: Do you often blur out answers before questions have been completed?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really think I do that.
